## 2026-11-06 10:56 AM CT (16:56Z): W-SIMCOUNT1 (why the OICORE sim gives 548, 549 or 478 triggers): band edge rounding, the maintained sim matches the bot

- Descriptive, try count 0 (w_simcount/). On oi/btc.jsonl, 548 triggers = the bot's rule; 549 = 548 plus one window that exists only in fwd_cache.jsonl (which oicore_nov13.py and w_oicore_rise/run.py merge in). 478 is the raw price scripts (w_oicore_slip an.py, oicore_hist cheap() which w_oicore_runs uses). The 70 window gap has one cause, band edge rounding: the bot ceils the YES ask to the cent and prices NO as 1 minus the floored bid (kalshiSlotRunLib.js near line 115), so a raw price of 0.04x becomes 0.05 and enters the band; the 70 split as 42 YES ask and 28 NO price. Other causes: missing c-120 candle 0, cheaper side or tie rule 0 (the side agrees in all 478 common windows), missing candles or OI 0; windows in the raw set but not the bot set 0. Reading: the maintained sim (548 or 549) is the bot's rule; the 478 figures in W-OICORE-SLIP1 (478 triggers, 26.8% win rate) and W-OICORE-RUNS1 (sim streaks) undercount by the 70 band edge windows (cheapest bucket), so their sim percentages are for a slightly different, dearer set; the live fills sit in the cheapest bucket because the bot's rule admits those edge windows. Nothing changed.

## 2026-11-06 10:56 AM CT (16:56Z): W-EVENT830-FEE1 (recorded fees against the formula): the gap is one sided and grows with order size

- Descriptive, try count 0 (w_event830_fee/; no fee field is recorded and avgFill is null, so the fee is implied: gross at the price field minus settledPnl). EVENT830: all 5 fills are 1 contract losses; implied fee equals ceil to cent 0.07*p*(1-p) on 5 of 5 (0.02, 0.02, 0.01, 0.01, 0.02); 1 contract cannot show a size effect. FLOW1 1 of 1 matches (4c). OICORE (10 contracts): above the per order rounded formula on 28 of 47, equal on 19, never below; mean 12.8c vs 6.7c, +$2.88 in total. Gold L1 wins and losses (exits skipped): above on 182 of 272, equal on 90, never below; mean 8.2c vs 5.0c, +$8.67 in total. By size: gap 0 or near 0 at 1 contract (3 of 11 above), growing with size; at 10 contracts gold 16 of 18 above, OICORE 28 of 45 above; at 13 to 16 contracts 9 to 22 above per size; never negative at any size. Explanations: per order rounding would cap the gap at 1c, so it is not that; a per contract ceil fails too (overshoots gold, misses OICORE); a one sided gap that grows with size looks like a size scaling cost: a taker rate above 7%, an extra per contract fee, or fills worse than the price field (slippage vs the limit); this data cannot separate those. Implied fee is about 1.8x the unrounded formula on wins and 2.3x on losses. Reading: either the real fill price is worse than the stored price (consistent with the null avgFill and the sim vs live gap) or fees are above the sim's model; the bot's own recorded costs are above the formula the sim and the research use by about $11.5 over about 320 orders (a cost the research session's yardsticks do not include). The report fix avgFill: num(o.averageFillPrice) (already with main) would separate the two. Nothing changed.

Window cap 5.5% of base (25.6 at 466, 27.0 at 490, 28.5 at 518) cuts only against spend already in the same window, so a 12 to 15 dollar order passes; the 50 contract ceiling is irrelevant. ## 2026-11-05 10:37 AM CT (16:37Z): W-GLIMIT1 (gold limit versus seen quote): bar met, but it is a rounding artefact and a cushion would cost more than it earns

- Worker finished read only (w_goldlimit/REGISTRATION.md, try count 2, bar: permutation p under 0.025 AND a monotone no fill pattern). 416 gold orders, 8 with a null seen quote, 408 used (263 filled, 145 no fill). Gap in cents on the side bought (YES buy limit minus seenAsk; NO buy seenBid minus limit): filled n 263, median 0.3c, 30.4% with gap 0 or less; no fill n 145, median 0.1c, 46.2% with gap 0 or less; permutation p 0.0001 (10,000 draws, seed 20261105). No fill rate by bucket (Wilson 95%): gap 0 or less 41.6% (116 of 279, 36 to 47%); gap 1c 22.5% (29 of 129, 16 to 30%); gap 2c or more: empty, so monotone rests on two buckets. Bar met with that caveat.
- Code rule (origin/main kalshiLiveLib.js): YES buy limit = ceil(ask to the cent) (l1Pick lines 605 to 606); NO buy limit = floor(bid to the cent) (lines 610 to 611); the order is IOC (line 190). So the gap is always under 1c by construction (0.0 to 0.9c), a sub cent rounding artefact: when the quote is exactly a whole cent the limit equals the touch and there is no cushion, and 42% of those miss, against 22% when the quote has a sub cent part.
- Why I am not proposing a cushion (my arithmetic, not the worker's): gold's per contract edge today is about 4.85% x -75.8c + 95.15% x 6.22c = +2.24c on filled orders; the missed orders look ordinary (they would have won 93.3%). A flat +1c limit slip, like the slot's SLOT_LIMIT_SLIP, would cost 1c on every fill, cutting the edge to about +1.24c; for it to pay, fills per signal would have to rise by more than 2.24 / 1.24 = 1.8 times, which is impossible from a 65% fill rate. A slip only on a retry order would be a different design (two orders, new duplicate order risk) and is for main and the owner, not me. Not checked by the worker: the gap may proxy for price level or time of day (not tested against price band and hour). No rule change proposed and no shadow brief drafted.

The live entry compares the live market read (GET markets/ticker) at the minute tick, about 177 s before close, not the candle close. Price is rounded UP to a whole cent (yes = ceil(yes_ask*100)/100; no = 1 - floor(yes_bid*100)/100), the cheaper rounded side is bought, and that rounded price is checked against 0.05 to 0.50 and used as the IOC limit. A raw 0.041 to 0.049 rounds to 0.05 and passes, which is why live limits run 0.05 to 0.07. Replaying the rule on seenBid and seenAsk reproduces side and price for 43 of 43 live OICORE orders since Oct 30. The code does what CLAUDE.md says.
- The sim (forward_oicore.py) uses the raw c-180 candle ask, no cent rounding, no 177 s timing, and picks YES on an exact tie where the code picks NO. That accounts for the 9 live only fills; the Oct 31 NO at 0.50 was not a tie (live saw bid 0.50 ask 0.51, a one cent move).
- 6 sim triggers have no live order and no no fill document: Nov 1 14:45Z, Nov 2 14:45Z, 15:45Z, 17:00Z, Nov 3 17:30Z, 23:00Z. All unexplained. Ruled out where visible: kill latch, daily and lifetime caps (net positive), L1 window use, depth share (cuts counts, never skips), OI margin (large except Nov 2 17:00, 86,523 vs 86,300). ## 2026-11-04 02:42 AM CT (08:42Z): W101 what clamps OICORE orders, and an implied sizing base

- W101 (from origin/main `kalshiSlotLib.js`; the base is not in the code): OICORE is 10 contracts, stepped down one at a time until the cost fits all of: per order 1% of base (max $20); per window 2% of base minus slot dollars already in the window; open total 3% of base minus open slot dollars; window room min(5.5% x base - L1 spend - slot spend - $1.05, 2.75% x base - slot spend); daily room 2% of base plus the day's worst net; lifetime room 6% of base plus the lifetime worst net. Also depth share 25% of the touch size and open contracts per market at most 10. The 9 at 0.50: 10 would cost about $5.18 (including fee) and 9 about $4.66, so if the per-order cap bound, the base is about $466 to $518 (my W73 guess of $500 sits inside it). The 2 at 0.14: 10 would cost about $1.49, 2 about $0.30, 3 about $0.45, so the binding room was only $0.30 to $0.45; a per-order cap on a $490 base is about $4.9, so something else bound: window room (L1 or slot dollars already open in that window), open-total room or a daily or lifetime room used up. Cannot separate these without the order timestamps and other open orders. Consequence for the read: dearer entries (30c to 50c) are the ones clamped below 10 contracts, so per-contract numbers are the fair comparison and dollar totals understate the dear end. With a base of about $490, the worst rolling 24 hours (-$35.61, W73) is about -7.3% against the 12% stop (about $59), and the OICORE per order cap is about $4.9.

## 2026-11-04 02:09 AM CT (08:09Z): W62 OICORE no-fills were not thin books

- W62 (public trade tape, all 8 unfilled OICORE orders; hypothesis stated first: the book had no resting size at the order price): NOT SUPPORTED. Taker buys of our side at or below the order price came within about 1 s of the order time for 5 of 8 (250 to 5,234 contracts: Oct 31 18:30, Nov 1 09:00, 09:15, 23:30, Nov 3 17:30). Unclear for 3: Nov 1 11:15 had no taker buys of our side (fits a thin quote), Oct 30 21:30 first hit at 58 s, Nov 3 21:00 first hit at 44 s. Caveat: trades at the price do not prove our order was at the front. So most OICORE no-fills are not explained by an empty book; the order either arrived after the size was gone or was rejected or timed out for a reason the order record does not carry. Open question for the code owner (the order record gives only "no fill"): which of IOC expiry, late arrival or rejection. Not a strategy question; no change proposed. Also note the earlier W60 point that 6 of 8 had a lower later ask than the order price fits an order that missed the quote's moment, not a thin book.

## 2026-11-04 02:08 AM CT (08:08Z): W67 power of the Nov 13 OICORE read

- W67 (bootstrap, 5000 draws; edge real at the full historical mean +7.73c / half the mean +3.87c): n 40: P(PF >= 1.5) 0.67 / 0.36, P(PF >= 1.0 or net > 0) 0.90 / 0.72; n 55: 0.70 / 0.36, 0.94 / 0.77; n 100: 0.78 / 0.32, 0.98 / 0.84. Reading: even if the edge is fully real, a PF >= 1.5 read at 40 to 55 fills fails about 30% of the time, and it barely separates a real edge from a half-size one (0.70 vs 0.36). The sign of net separates them better only at larger n. So the Nov 13 read should be set up as: net > 0 is the minimum, PF >= 1.5 is not a pass/fail bar at 55 fills, and a miss is not a kill (the registered kills stay the 18-streak and net below -$6 after 40 fills). Decision for the owner/main, not mine: whether to extend the read to 100 fills (about Nov 18, if fills run near the trigger rate).

## 2026-11-04 01:59 AM CT (07:59Z): W52 why gold orders do not fill

- W52 (descriptive; 78 gold no-fill orders since Oct 21, W49's 56 was in-band windows only): the bot's own seen ask was at or below the order price in all 78, and the book had size in all 78. The candle ask after the send was above the order price in 61 of 69 windows (88%). Cause: the ask rose between the bot's read and the order reaching Kalshi, so the immediate-or-cancel limit found nothing. If filled at the order price, the 69 windows with results would have won 95.7%, +3.04c/ct after fee. Caveat that matters: those are the windows where price ROSE, so a fill at the new, higher ask earns less than +3.04c, and the unfilled set is selected by the move itself. So this is the ceiling of what a looser limit could add, not an estimate. It is the same adverse-selection shape as the OICORE S-LIM1-LIVE tag read; gold has no live test of a looser limit. Possible pre-registered brief for the owner (not sent, not live): gold limit +1c over the seen ask, read on tagged fills at 40. Hold until S-LIM1-LIVE reads (OICORE tagged 5 of 20).

## 2026-11-04 01:59 AM CT (07:59Z): W51 why my sim misses 7 live OICORE orders

- W51: 7 live OICORE orders since Oct 30 12:00Z (6 fills, 1 no-fill) have no sim trigger. OIR passed in all 7 (87k to 244k) and candles were present. The only failed condition is my cheap-side ask at the close-180 candle: 0.015 to 0.044, all under 0.05. The bot saw 0.041 to 0.067 about 162 to 177 s before close and its limit was ask+1c (0.05 to 0.07). Cause: the bot reads the price 5 to 20 s after my candle, and prices of 0.04 to 0.05 are common; the band appears to be applied to the limit (ask+1c), so asks of 0.04 to 0.049 still qualify, while my sim needs ask >= 0.05. My sim is stricter at the 0.05 edge, so its sim table (31 windows) undercounts live triggers by about 7. Orders carry no oiRise field, so the bot's OIR cannot be compared. ### Nov 3, 2026, 6:32 AM CT (12:32Z): what do L1's losses have in common? (asked by main for the owner; read only)

Data: live gold and Dogecoin settled fills since Oct 20 (346 fills, 21 early sells = 6.1%, plus 2 settle-against losses) and the 62 day candle history (gold 1,179 and Dogecoin 1,776 in-band windows, 179 early sells = 6.1%, no settle-against). Outcome scored: early sell at the 25c line. Dimensions fixed before looking: side, price, hour of day (CT), weekday, rise3, rng5, spread, move into the entry in the last minute, time since the last early sell, early sells among the previous 4 windows, and (live only) depth at the limit and share of the touch. Tries: **147 bucket comparisons** (37 live, 110 history in gold, Doge and pooled), so about 7 would reach p under 0.05 by chance and a Bonferroni line is p under 0.0003.

**What does NOT separate losses (all noise or opposite):**
- **Side:** NO 6.1% (12 of 197) against YES 6.0% (9 of 149) live; NO 6.1% against YES 6.0% pooled in history (p 0.88). The five recent NO losses are a recent cluster, not a rule.
- **Hour and weekday:** 5.3% to 6.6% by six-hour block in history, no pattern; live Monday 15% (8 of 53, p 0.007) does not appear in history (Monday 6.9%).
- **rise3 and rng5:** history quartiles are flat (5.1% to 6.8%, p over 0.2); live rise3 under 0 loses 13.5% (5 of 37, p 0.051), which does not replicate in history. Not a rule.
- **Spread:** 5.8% at 1c or less, 7.6% above (p 0.14). **Hours since the last early sell:** no pattern. **Early sells in the previous 4 windows:** 0 earlier = 6.7%, 1 or more = 4.0% (p 0.010): if anything the opposite of clustering.
- **Depth and share of the touch (live only):** order larger than the touch 16% (4 of 25, p 0.08), otherwise 0% to 7%. Not significant.
- **Move into the entry in the last minute (history):** the biggest rise into the entry loses 8.1% against 4.2% to 6.6% for the rest (pooled p 0.012, Dogecoin 0.012, gold same direction, 0.31): a mean reversion story, but it does not survive the 147 count.

**What survives: PRICE.** Early sells fall steadily with the price paid, in both series and in the live record:
| Price | History windows | Early sells | Net per contract (first / second half) | Share of all early sells |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 88 to 90c | 477 | 10.5% | +1.07c (+0.55 / +1.55) | 28% |
| 90 to 92c | 721 | 9.8% | **-0.43c (+0.23 / -1.13)** | 40% |
| 92 to 94c | 618 | 4.5% | +2.42c (+1.93 / +2.88) | 16% |
| 94 to 96c | 691 | 2.7% | +2.13c (+1.30 / +2.95) | 11% |
| 96 to 97c | 448 | 2.5% | +1.02c (+0.60 / +1.49) | 6% |
Pooled p is under 0.0003 for four of the five buckets, the same shape in gold (10.0, 8.7, 5.1, 1.5, 3.7%) and Dogecoin (10.8, 10.6, 4.1, 3.5, 1.7%), and the live record agrees on the main points (90 to 92c lost 13.0%, 12 of 92; 92 to 96c lost 1 of 149; the same bucket nets -$21.32 live). The mechanism is the obvious one: a cheaper favourite is less likely to hold, and 88 to 92c is where the win (7 to 10c) fails to pay for the 80c loss often enough. **The 90 to 92c bucket is the only one with a negative net per contract**, 40% of all early sells from 24% of windows. That is C1's cell, found on the live fills and now seen independently on 721 history windows (history gets no credit toward adopting a filter, README P1; the forward read stays at 80 settled cell fills, currently 15 settled, 3 losses, net -$12.25). Skipping 90 to 92c in history would raise the net per contract from +1.23c to +1.76c on 76% of the windows; skipping 88 to 92c gives +1.95c on 59% but a smaller total (the 88 to 90c bucket is positive, +1.07c). So nothing NEW to register: C1 is the rule the data points to, and the data does not support widening it to 88 to 90c.

**Loss runs and dollars:** longest run of consecutive losing fills live: 2 (-$17.79); no longer run, and no loss clustering by the prior-loss test above. Loss dollars by US Central day (net / loss dollars / early sells): Oct 21 -$6.25 / -$11.79 / 4; Oct 25 -$3.93 / -$9.58 / 1; Oct 26 -$7.26 / -$19.33 / 3; Oct 27 -$0.09 / -$11.39 / 2; Oct 30 +$2.14 / -$12.77 / 2; Oct 31 +$3.51 / -$8.70 / 1; **Nov 2 -$41.42 / -$55.32 / 6** (Dogecoin 04:09 94.2c -$11.47, 14:54 90.4c -$5.51, 18:09 90.7c -$10.22, 19:54 96.1c -$8.48, 23:39 90.2c -$10.33, and gold 20:24 89.0c -$9.31, all US Central, four of them in the 88 to 92c range); Nov 3 so far -$1.00 / -$9.64 / 1. Nov 2 is the worst day on record by a factor of 5 (the previous worst was -$7.26): about 5% of a $779 base or 8% of $540. **Live, the 90 to 92c bucket alone is 92 fills with net -$21.32 against +$31.68 for the other 254 fills, and holds 12 of the 21 early sells (-$74.49 of -$131.77).** Skipping it would have turned Nov 2 from -$41.42 into -$20.58 (still a bad day) and the whole record from +$10.36 to +$31.68, at the cost of 27% of the fills. That is in-sample (the live fills are where C1 came from), so it adds nothing to the case for adoption; the forward read stays at 80.

### Nov 2, 2026, 20:24 PM CT (02:24Z Nov 3): Dogecoin ladder record 5 losses in 51 (9.8%, net -$9.77); C8's absolute threshold has gone stale and the lead is weaker than it looked

**Dogecoin at ladder size (130 hour window from Oct 30 8 PM CT): 51 settled fills, 5 losses (9.8%), net -$9.77, -1.44c a contract.** The fifth loss is in the window since my last note; the review rule (last 100 settled lose more than 8% or net under +0.5c a contract) reads at 100 and has not fired, but both lines are now crossed in this half-sample. Gold at ladder size: 47 fills, 1 loss. The candle engine on the Dogecoin windows that closed after 17:10Z (C8's forward read) agrees: **4 early sells in the 11 in-band windows (36%)** against 6.2% over the 62 day history (the chance of 4 or more in 11 at 6.2% is about 0.5%, before counting that I looked because it was bad). Dogecoin is in a bad stretch both live and in the engine.

**C8 is stale in its own units.** Dogecoin's open interest has doubled: median open interest at the 9 minute candle 3,302 contracts in the first 14 days of the history, 7,251 in the last 14, 8,664 in the forward windows. The open interest rise grew with it (median in-band rise 1,168 first 14 days, 2,849 last 14). My fixed threshold of 755 contracts caught 30% of windows over the history but only 19% of forward windows, and **0 of the 11 forward in-band windows** (the smallest in-band rise was 831). So C8 as registered will almost never fire and cannot reach 150 low windows.
Scale-free versions, computed now (descriptive, counted as 5 more tries, N about 728,831):
- Relative rise (the 3 minute rise over open interest at 9 minutes, fixed at the discovery half's 20th percentile, 0.2478): discovery 178 windows lost 8.4% against 5.5% (p 0.10); validation 202 windows 9.9% against 5.2% (p 0.016); net -1.38c against +1.92c. Weaker than the absolute cut (p 0.0016) and the share of forward windows below it is 35%, so it also drifts.
- Walk-forward rolling cut (the 20th percentile of the relative rise over the previous 14 days of all windows): 159 low windows, lost 9.4% against 6.4% (p 0.10); first half no effect (7.6% against 6.5%), second half 11.2% against 6.4% (p 0.085).
**Reading:** the low open interest lead shrinks from strong to weak once the scale is controlled, and it was concentrated in the second half. I downgrade it to a weak lead. I do not start a C8b: no variant of it is solid enough to spend a forward slot on. C8 keeps counting (it costs nothing) but will not be informative; I stop reading it each wake after the next two reads unless the low arm starts filling.

Told main (drift flag threshold: net below -$5 and loss rate near 10%).

### Nov 2, 2026, 15:53 PM CT (21:53Z): C9 REGISTERED BEFORE COUNTING: forward shadow read of L1's own rule on SILVER (KXSILVER15M). Public candles only, no code, no change to orders. Counts windows that close after 22:00Z Nov 2.

Requested by main at the owner's direction (approved). Silver was the only near-miss in C7 (+1.09c a contract, lost share 6.5%, 125 in-band windows a week, 1.0c spread, 173 contracts at the touch).

**Rule (exactly as used in C7):** at the 1 minute candle that ends 360 s before the close, buy the side priced 0.88 to 0.97 (YES at the YES ask close, NO at 1 minus the YES bid close); hold; early sell at the first later 1 minute candle whose bid close for the bought side is 0.25 or lower, at that bid close; fees 7% x price x (1 minus price) on entry and on an exit. **Tightened cost screen, as I said I would:** spread under 2c AND at least 100 contracts at the touch. Silver passes (1.0c, 173). The screen is fixed for this read; if Silver's spread or touch drifts out of it I say so.

**Overlap with gold and Dogecoin, from the 62 day history (descriptive, computed before counting):**
- 49% of Silver's in-band windows (546 of 1,104) are also in band for gold, 32% (354) for Dogecoin, 17% (187) for all three. 46% of gold's in-band windows have Silver in band as well.
- **Losses cluster:** in the 546 shared Silver and gold windows, gold lost 27 and Silver lost 27; **9 of gold's 27 losses (33%) coincided with a Silver loss**, against 3.5% for Silver losing when gold did not. A gold loss would come with a Silver loss a third of the time, so adding Silver adds risk that is NOT independent.
- **The shared 5.5% window cap (limit = 5.5% of the sizing base):** at 12 or 15 contracts each, gold + Silver cost $22 to $28 in a window, under the cap at either base ($29.7 at a $540 base, $42.8 at $779), so the cap would never have cut Silver alone with gold. With Dogecoin as well (three orders, 187 windows, 17% of Silver's), the cap at $540 is exceeded in all 187 (cost $33 to $42 against $29.7), so at that base Silver would crowd out or shrink the third order in about one Silver window in six; at $779 the cap is exceeded in 0 windows at 12 contracts and 16 at 15 contracts. The 12% stop (about $65 to $93) is more than a typical three-way loss only if more than 4 to 6 orders in a row stop out, but a three-way loss window (gold + Silver + Dogecoin all stopping) would cost $30 to $42, about half of the $540-base stop. In history, in those 187 three-way windows, all three lost together 0 times and any two lost together 5 times.

**Forward sample size:** 400 in-band Silver windows (about 3.2 weeks at 125 a week). A look at 200 (about 1.6 weeks). Read the lost share first; the net per contract is too noisy to confirm anything in weeks: its standard error is about 1.4c at 250 windows, 1.1c at 400, and 0.5c needs about 1,900 windows (15 weeks). So this read can KILL Silver quickly if it is clearly worse than gold, but it can only say "consistent with gold" about profit, not prove it.

**Prediction (stated before):** over 400 forward in-band windows, lost share at most 8% (history 6.5%; the break-even loss share in this engine is near 8%) and net per contract at least +0.5c (history +1.09c; the simulator is about 1 to 1.7c more pessimistic than live). Silver's loss rate in windows shared with a gold in-band window is expected to stay near the history 5% when gold wins and 33% when gold loses.

**Kill rules:** at 200 windows, dead if the lost share is above 12% or the net per contract is below -2.0c. At 400 windows, dead if the lost share is above 10% or the net per contract is below -1.0c. If neither kill nor the prediction, the read is inconclusive and extends by 400 windows (a second look, counted).

**Cost to N:** 1 registered test (1 series, 2 looks) = about 3 tries, N about 728,824. A pass here earns a PROPOSAL to the owner (Silver as a second metals market under the same window cap, size, 12% stop and 25c exit), never anything live from me. If proposed, the correlation above must be weighed: adding Silver raises the number of windows with an order from about 1.0 to about 1.5 a window for gold (46% of gold windows) and doubles the loss in the 33% joint cases.

**Counting:** `c9_fwd.py` (scratchpad) reads each newly settled Silver (and gold, for the overlap) window once from the public candles and prints the read; run each wake. Counting starts with the first window that closes after 22:00:00Z Nov 2. Nothing else changes.

### Nov 2, 2026, 15:50 PM CT (21:50Z): C7 RESULT: L1's own rule on 11 untested 15 minute series. NO SERIES MEETS THE PASS BAR. Silver is the only near-miss. History only; nothing live.

Registration: the 16:00Z entry above (fixed before any outcome was read). History: up to 62 days (Sep 1 to Nov 2) of public 1 minute candles; metals Palladium and Platinum and the forex pairs list only 40 to 46 days (Kalshi lists them from later). All losses in this engine are early sells at the 25c line (settle-against is 0.0% everywhere except Palladium 0.1% and Platinum 0.1%), as in the live record.

**Calibration (same code, gold and Dogecoin against their live results):** gold sim +1.42c a contract [+0.15, +2.68] against +2.3c live; Dogecoin +1.10c [+0.09, +2.11] against +2.8c live. Differences of 0.9c and 1.7c, inside the 2c void line: the simulator is usable, a little pessimistic (the candle close proxy catches about 1.5 times more early sells than the live 10 second watcher, 5.9% and 6.2% against 4.1%). Note the pass bar I set (net at least +1.0c in both halves) is stricter than gold's own simulated result (+0.65c in the first half), so a series can miss the bar and still look like gold.

**Per series (ranked by cost, spread ascending then touch size descending):**

Trading around the clock: every series has windows in all 24 UTC hours on weekdays. Crypto series trade 7 days a week; metals and forex trade Monday to Friday plus a short Sunday evening and (metals) Friday evening reopening: Silver and Copper have 51 to 54 windows on each of Saturday and Sunday against about 800 on a weekday; Palladium, Platinum and the three forex pairs have none on Saturday and 38 to 56 on Sunday. The forex touch size (562 contracts on all three) is one repeated number in my 16 samples: a market maker quote of the same size on all three, so treat it as 'deep' but not as a measured depth.

**Registered checks:**
- Cost screen predicted at most 3 passes; **4 passed** (Silver and the three forex pairs). Per the registration the screen is therefore too loose: it let through three pairs that lose 2.5c to 5.5c a contract, because a 2.0c spread sits right at the 2c limit. A tighter screen (spread under 2c) would leave Silver alone.
- Pass bar: **0 of 11** (none has net at least +1.0c in both halves with the lower bound above zero). Expected passes from noise alone across the 11, under the no-edge-at-quoted-price null: **0.29**. Silver's first half (+0.56c) and its interval lower bound (-0.25c) are what stops it.
- Forex: all three negative with intervals clear of zero (EURUSD, GBPUSD, USDJPY): 10% to 13% of in-band windows end in an early sell, against 6% for gold and Dogecoin. They are the opposite of a lead.
- Crypto (HYPE, BNB, Zcash, NEAR): net -0.4c to -1.2c, lost share 8% to 9%: the rule does not work as well on the new altcoins as on Dogecoin; touch sizes 25 to 88 contracts (below 100 on all four) so a 12 to 15 contract order would be 14% to 50% of the touch.
- Metals: Copper, Palladium and Platinum have thin books (18 to 61 contracts at the touch) and spreads of 2 to 3c; Silver is the exception (1.0c spread, 173 contracts) and is the only series with a result close to gold's.

**What this means:** no series qualifies for a forward shadow read under the registered bar. **Silver** is worth naming: simulated net +1.09c (gold +1.42c), lost share 6.5% (gold 5.9%), 125 in-band windows a week (gold 133), a 1.0c spread and a touch deeper than Dogecoin's. It is a near-twin of gold in this engine, and that is the only reason it is a candidate for a watch, not a finding; its interval includes zero. Two cautions before anyone proposes it: (1) silver and gold move together, so a Silver order in the same window as a gold order adds the same risk once more against the 5.5% window cap and the 12% stop (the 'one window, one limit' rule); (2) it would add about 125 in-band windows a week, about as many as gold itself, so it is a frequency lever, but also a doubling of metals exposure.
**Tries:** the 36 registered. N about 728,821 after the C6/C8 counts (728,785 + 36).

The simulator is a little pessimistic (about 1 to 1.7c a contract), not off by 2c in the wrong way. - *Evidence, descriptive only:* Dogecoin windows with OIR at or under 755.1 contracts (the discovery half's 20th percentile, fixed blind to outcomes): discovery 178 windows, lost share 9.6%, net -1.10c a contract, against 5.2% and +1.57c for the rest (Fisher p 0.027); validation 97 windows, 14.4% and -5.40c against 5.3% and +1.98c for the rest (p 0.0016). It holds in both halves. Counted against the 28 registered cells that is about p 0.045 for the validation half: a lead, not a finding. Gold shows nothing (bottom 20%: 3.4% / 5.9% lost, no effect).
- *Rule (fixed now):* Dogecoin only. At the 6 minute look, OIR = open interest of the candle that ENDS 360 s before the close minus that of the candle that ENDS 540 s before. SKIP when OIR is under 755.12 contracts (a number, not refit later). Missing candles: no skip decision (L1 unchanged), counted.
- *Forward shadow read, no code and no change in live orders:* score every in-band Dogecoin window whose close is after 2026-11-02T17:10Z from the public candles (all windows, not only the bot's fills), with the same engine as C6/C7. Sample: 150 low-OIR windows against the rest (about 600), at about 40 low windows a week, so 3.7 weeks.
- *Prediction:* low-OIR lost share at least 9% and at least 1.5 times the rest, net per contract under 0 in the low arm, one sided Fisher p under 0.05. Power by simulation (150 against 600): 73% if the true rates are 11.5% against 5.3%, 47% if 9.6% against 5.3%, false pass 2%.
- *Kill:* at 150 low-OIR windows, dead if the low arm's lost share is not above the rest's, or its net per contract is above +1.0c. Early kill at 75 low windows if zero or one early sells and net above +2.0c.
- *Cost if it ever went live:* 20% of Dogecoin's in-band entries (about 40 of 200 simulated windows a week, which is about 18 of the bot's roughly 88 Dogecoin fills a week), about 12% of all L1 fills. History says those fills lose money on average (-1.1c and -5.4c), so the cost is frequency, not profit. Frequency stays a hard requirement: a PASS goes to the owner as a trade, not a rule.
- *Order of scoring:* C1 (90 to 92c) is the forward test already running on the bot's own fills; C8 scores from public candles and does not use the bot's orders, so it can run beside C1 without interfering. Main said one new L1 filter forward at a time: C8 is a second filter, so it waits for the owner's word before I start counting; the registration date above is the earliest date it can count from.

### Nov 2, 2026, 10:00 AM CT (16:00Z; first version said 10:10, a clock slip): C7 REGISTERED BEFORE ANY SCORING: L1's own rule on 11 untested 15 minute series (history only). Nothing live.

Request: main, at the owner's direction (V2 found many 15 minute series never tested with L1's rule). Runs after C6's data pull finishes; the cost screen and the book sampler start now because they use no outcomes.

**Series (all 11 named by main, fixed now):** KXSILVER15M, KXCOPPER15M, KXPALLADIUM15M, KXPLATINUM15M (metals), KXHYPE15M, KXBNB15M, KXZEC15M, KXNEAR15M (crypto), KXEURUSD15M, KXGBPUSD15M, KXUSDJPY15M (forex). Gold (KXGOLD15M) and Dogecoin (KXDOGE15M) are run through the SAME code as the calibration (the simulated net per contract should land near their live record, +2.3c gold and +2.8c Dogecoin, else the simulator is wrong and I say so). The tickers exist in Kalshi's public series list (checked); I add no other series.

**The rule (L1's own, unchanged):** at the 1 minute candle that ENDS 360 s before the close, buy the side whose price is 0.88 to 0.97 (YES at the YES ask close; NO at 1 minus the YES bid close); hold to settlement; early sell at the first later 1 minute candle whose bid close for the bought side is 0.25 or lower, at that bid close; fees 7% x price x (1 minus price) on entry and on an exit. Same definitions as C6 (`oi_an.py`'s engine without the open interest feature). Windows with a missing candle at 360 s, no result, or out of band are excluded and counted.

**Universe chosen on cost, not returns (fixed before any outcome is read):** the 11 series are screened on two cost numbers measured WITHOUT outcomes: (1) the median spread (YES ask close minus YES bid close) at the 6 minute candle over all windows; (2) the median top-of-book size on the bought side, from live sampling of the open market at about 6 minutes before each close (a background sampler, every 15 minutes for several hours, reads the public market object: `yes_bid_size_fp` and `yes_ask_size_fp`, and the live bid/ask). Screen: **passes cost if median spread is 2c or less AND median top-of-book size is at least 100 contracts** (gold and Dogecoin live orders carry a median 54 to 100; a 12 contract order is then at most about 12% of the touch). A series that fails the screen is still reported but cannot be proposed for a forward shadow read. Ranking in the report: spread ascending, then size descending.

**Reported per series:** windows in the history, windows in band, in-band windows per week, the share of the 24 hours (UTC) covered by windows and the days they trade (round the clock or not), loss share (early sell, settle against, and combined), net per contract after fees (with a 95% interval), the first-half and second-half values, the dollar cost of a loss at 12 and 16 contracts (the ladder sizes now and soon: the mean early-sell loss per contract x 12 and x 16), and the cost numbers above.

**Pass bar (set now):** a series is a **candidate for a forward shadow read** only if ALL hold: passes the cost screen; at least 300 in-band windows; net per contract at least +1.0c in BOTH halves (by close time); combined lost share at most 8% in both halves; and the lower 95% bound of the whole-history net per contract is above zero. It earns a forward shadow read and nothing live (README P1: history cannot count toward a rule). With 11 series the chance that one passes by luck is not small; I will estimate it by permuting the outcome-given-price within each series (the null of no edge at the quoted price) and say how many passes to expect from noise.

**Prediction (stated before, a base rate):** most of the 11 fail. The expected result is net per contract near minus the fees (about -0.3c to +1c) for the thin forex pairs and metals with wide spreads; the screen passes at most 3 of the 11. If more than 3 pass the screen I treat the screen as too loose.

**Kill (study level):** if the calibration on gold and Dogecoin lands more than 2c a contract away from their live results, the study is void until the simulator is fixed.

**Count for deflation:** 11 series x 2 halves, 11 cost cells, 2 calibration cells, 1 permutation null = about 36 tries; N about 728,781 (after the C6 count of about 28). The Bonferroni bar stays about z 5.2: any pass is a lead for a shadow read, never a finding.

**Sampling plan:** history from the public candles (about 60 days where it exists; shorter for newer series), pulled after C6 at one read per window with 0.4 s spacing (about 5,000 windows a series for the 24/7 series, fewer for metals and forex), so several hours of pulling; I will report in stages.

### Nov 2, 2026, 8:35 AM CT (14:35Z; the first version of this heading said 9:55 AM, a clock slip, fixed): C6 REGISTERED BEFORE ANY SCORING: open interest rise as a SKIP on L1 entries (gold, Dogecoin). History test only; no forward scoring until C1 finishes. Nothing live.

Source of the idea: the owner, via main ("use an open-interest condition like OICORE's, but as a SKIP on L1 entries, to avoid favourites that flip"). Checked against what is known: V2's EXITOI-G (open-interest exit for L1) was falsified pooled; V2's NEWH3 on Bitcoin found FALLING open interest 3 to 6 minutes before the close marked failing favourites (opposite direction, lead only). Neither tested a RISING open interest skip at entry on gold or Dogecoin. 16 of L1's 18 live losses are early sells at the 25c line, so I score the early sell and the settle-against separately and name the combined one.

**Data (checked before registering, not scored):** Kalshi's public candlesticks (`/series/{series}/markets/{ticker}/candlesticks`, 1 minute, no key) carry `open_interest_fp` for both KXGOLD15M and KXDOGE15M, back to at least Sep 3, 2026 (about 60 days) for both; they also carry `yes_bid` and `yes_ask` close, high and low. The market result is in the settled market list. I use only these public fields (the same `open_interest_fp` OICORE reads), through plain GETs spaced at least 0.4 s with backoff on 429.

**The rule (one rule, fixed now):**
- *Feature:* OIR = `open_interest_fp` of the candle that ENDS 360 s before the close minus that of the candle that ENDS 540 s before the close (the 3 minutes up to the 6 minute look; OICORE's window is 180 to 360 s before the close, which is after L1's look and could not be used at entry). A missing candle or value excludes the window from both arms and is counted.
- *Universe:* every settled window of the series in the history where, at the candle ending 360 s before the close, the YES ask close is in [0.88, 0.97] (buy YES) or 1 minus the YES bid close is in [0.88, 0.97] (buy NO). Decided from that one candle, before the outcome (not from anything later, the G3H1 lesson).
- *Threshold:* per series, the 80th percentile of OIR among in-band windows of the DISCOVERY half (the earlier half of the days by close time), fixed from the feature alone and never looked at against outcomes. SKIP when OIR is at or above the threshold. Applied unchanged to the VALIDATION half (the later half). 20% of in-band entries are skipped by construction.
- *Outcomes, per window, entered at the in-band price, 1 contract:* settle against = the market result is the other side. Early sell = the bought side's bid CLOSE at any 1 minute candle after entry up to the close is 25c or lower (YES: yes_bid close; NO: 1 minus yes_ask close); the exit price is that candle's bid close. Net per contract = held win 1 minus price, settle loss minus price, early sell exit bid minus price, less 0.07 x price x (1 minus price) at entry and the same on an exit. Lost share = early sell or settle against, over all entered windows. Calibration check before reading anything: the simulated early sell rate over ALL in-band windows should be near the live 4% (16 of 393); if it is more than twice that, the exit proxy is wrong and I say so.

**Prediction (stated before):** in the VALIDATION half, per series and pooled: (a) skipped windows have a lost share at least 1.5 times the kept windows' and at least 5%; (b) the skipped arm's net per contract is under +0.5c, while the kept arm is above +1.0c; (c) the one sided exact test of the lost share (skipped above kept) has p under 0.05, tested separately for gold and Dogecoin (2 tests). All three in both series would be a PASS of the history test.

**Kill rules (history test):** dead for a series if its skipped arm's lost share is not above the kept arm's in validation, or its skipped arm's net per contract is above +1.5c. A pass on history does NOT make it live and does not count toward adoption (README P1: history cannot count toward a filter). It only earns a forward shadow read after C1, with a kill rule set then.

**Also reported, labelled descriptive:** the discovery half and the pooled result; the BOTTOM 20% of OIR (to check V2's NEWH3 direction on these markets, counted as a try); the lost share by OIR decile (a dose-response check, 10 cells counted as tries); the frequency cost (windows skipped per week).

**Count for deflation:** 2 primary tests (gold, Dogecoin) + 1 pooled + 2 bottom tail + 20 decile cells + 3 calibration = about 28 tries, N about 728,745 after the C1 to C5 entries (728,683) and the two reviews (about 22). The Bonferroni bar stays about z 5.2, so a history pass here is a lead for a forward test, not a finding. Power will be stated from the counts once the windows are pulled (about 1,500 to 3,000 in-band windows per series is expected from 60 days).

Orders per week: if forward-tested, 20% of in-band entries (about 30 a week of 148) would be skipped; frequency cost stated here because it is a hard requirement.

### Nov 1, 2026, 5:55 PM CT (2026-11-01T22:55Z): C1 REGISTERED for forward shadow scoring (skip 90 to 92c entries). Nothing live. Scored ONLY on orders placed after this time.

Owner picked C1 (via main). This entry fixes everything before a single forward order is read. The 391 fills that suggested the idea are NOT part of the test.

**The one cell:** gold and Dogecoin L1 fills whose `price` (the price of the bought side at the 6 minute look, as logged on the order record) is at least 0.90 and under 0.92, pooled across the two markets. No other cell, no other split, no sub-hour or size cut is tested; any other cut I look at is a new try added to N.

**Scored set:** orders with `ts` after 2026-11-01T22:55:00Z, ticker starting KXGOLD or KXDOGE, `count` above 0 (filled) and `settledPnl` present (settled). Unfilled orders and open orders are not counted. An early sell at the 25c line is a loss (negative `settledPnl`), a stop-out is not removed. Comparison group ("the rest"): fills in the same period, same markets, with `price` under 0.90 or 0.92 and over. Net per contract = sum of `settledPnl` over sum of `count`. Nothing in the live bot changes: the cell keeps trading and this is just a counter on its orders.

**Prediction (stated before):** over 80 settled forward fills in the cell, (a) the cell's loss rate is at least 7% (in the 391-fill history it was 9.7%), (b) its net is under +0.5c a contract (history -0.1c pooled), and (c) the rest of the book's loss rate is under 4.5% (history 3.0%), with the one-sided Fisher exact p for cell loss rate above the rest's under 0.05. All three together is a PASS of the shadow test.

**Sample needed:** 80 settled forward fills in the cell. At the last 14 days' rate that is 38 cell fills a week (Dogecoin 23, gold 15), so about 2.1 weeks. Gold is closed until Monday Nov 2 00:00Z (6 PM CST Sunday), so the first hours are Dogecoin only; I count both markets as they come. One read at 80; no peeking for adoption. I will glance at 40 only for the early kill below.

**Power and false alarms (simulated, 3,000 runs each, 80 cell fills against 240 others):** if the true rates are the history's (9.7% cell, 3.0% rest) a PASS has a 60% chance; if the cell is only 6% against 3% it has an 18% chance; if there is no difference (4.5% both) a false PASS has a 2% chance. So a miss does not prove the idea wrong, and I will say so.

**Kill rules (dead = stop scoring, report):**
- At 80 settled cell fills: net above +1.5c a contract, or cell loss rate under 4%, or the Fisher p for cell loss rate above the rest above 0.30. Any of these = dead.
- Early kill at 40: zero losses in the cell AND net above +2.0c a contract (chance of that if the true loss rate were 9.7%: 1.7%).
- If neither kill nor PASS at 80, the result is INCONCLUSIVE and the cell gets one more block of 80 before a verdict (counted as a second look).

**Cost in fills (what skipping would give up):** 38 fills a week of about 148 (26%), Dogecoin 23 of 88, gold 15 of 60. In dollars the history says the cell's net was -$1.01 over 93 fills, so the profit given up is about zero; the cost is frequency and the dollars of fees and risk it would NOT take. Frequency is a hard requirement for L1, so a PASS here still goes to the owner as a trade between a quarter of the fills and about half of the loss events, not as a rule to ship.

**Deflation:** the cell was one of 10 price cells (5 bins by 2 markets) looked at in history, so the history p of 0.012 is about 0.12 after counting them. The forward test is a single pre-registered cell, so its p needs no further count; the shared N across all candidates stays about 728,683 and adds no new cells.

**Scoring recipe each wake (no scratch file needed):** nightly report with `orders=1`, filter as above, count cell fills, losses, net and contracts; log in this file only when the count crosses 40 or 80 or a kill triggers. Forward fills so far (after 5:55 PM CT Nov 1): 0.

### Nov 1, 2026, 3:55 PM CT: C3 (Dogecoin limit +1c) simulated on R6. Result: FAILS its registered kill rule on the current sample. No live change proposed.

Owner picked C3 (via main, shadow read only). Method, fixed before the numbers: Dogecoin ladder-size orders (ordered 11 or more) that carry send timing (`sentAtMs`, only from Oct 31 about 3:09 AM UTC) joined to the R6 `book15r6` Dogecoin book rows (2 second polls, files through Nov 1 16:27Z). For each order, take the last book snapshot before Kalshi's answer (median 0.5 s before the send), and count the size resting at the bought side's price levels within the order's worst price, then within the worst price plus 1c. Each contract fills at the book level's own price, so the extra cost of +1c is the level actually reached, not a flat cent. Fees: 7% of price times (1 minus price), rounded up per order. Loss size: the 25c line (a loss costs price minus 0.24 a contract, the observed Dogecoin exit average of about 69c).

Check that the model is sane before trusting it: of 30 joined orders, the pre-answer snapshot predicted fill or no fill correctly for 28 (19 fills and 9 no-fills right; 2 no-fills where the book showed size at the limit, so the real order was later than the snapshot). The post-answer snapshot is worse (5 fills it called empty), so the pre-answer one is used.

Sample: 30 of 45 ladder-size orders joined (the rest have no send timing, or no R6 row at the time), over 1.69 days; 11 were no-fills.

Result:
- The +1c limit would have turned **4 of those 11 no-fills into fills** (13, 13, 13 and 15 contracts, at 94.5c, 96.3c, 97.9c and 96.5c) and added **2 contracts and 1 contract to two partial fills** (93.1c and 97.1c). Another 7 no-fills stay empty (the book moved more than 1c, or the size was not there; 2 of them the model could not explain). So +1c reaches about 36% of no-fills, close to the "third" I predicted.
- Extra contracts: 57 in total, at an average price of **96.2c**. Two of the six extra fills are above 97c (97.9c and 97.1c), outside the band the entry rule judges on the seen price.
- All six extra fills won on this sample, for **+$2.00** in total. That is 6 wins and no losses, which says nothing about the loss rate.
- Expected value per extra contract, after the extra cent and fees, using the live exit loss size: **+1.21c at a 3.0% loss rate** (Dogecoin since it moved to the ladder size), **+0.07c at 4.5%** (all 176 Dogecoin fills, 8 losses), **-1.83c at 7.0%** (Dogecoin's 96 to 97c cell, 2 of 29). Break-even loss rate for a fill at 96.3c is 4.9%; at 97.9c it is 2.8%. Winning at 96 to 98c pays 2 to 4 cents; one stop-out erases 20 to 30 of them.
- Fills added: 4 no-fill conversions in 1.69 days. Scaled to a week that is about 17, but the base is 4 events; the count is more safely read as 13% of orders (4 of 30), which at 70 to 100 Dogecoin orders a week is 9 to 13 extra fills a week, plus 1 or 2 contracts on partials.

Against the registered rule ("dead if the simulated net per extra fill is under +0.5c a contract after the extra cent, or the cap on price removes the gain"): **net is +0.07c at the central loss rate, under the +0.5c bar; and the price cap removes the extra fills priced above 97c.** C3 is DEAD on this sample. It would be alive only if Dogecoin's loss rate at 95 to 98c is 3% or lower, which the 96 to 97c cell (7%) argues against. The no-fill share it was meant to fix is not free money: the fills that are missed are mostly the ones where the price ran up past the band's worth.

What this does NOT show: 30 orders is small (6 convertible events), the book is Kalshi's top 5 levels, and R6 files lag by hours so the newest orders are missing. I will re-run it once at 100 joined orders as a confirm (about Nov 8 to 10), not as a new test; if the loss-rate bar stays at 4.9% or above it stays dead. No live probe is needed or proposed. Cost to N: 1 simulation with 3 loss-rate cases, about 3 tries, N about 728,683.

The no-fill share (35%) is therefore mostly price moving away from the band, not a handling problem. C1, C2, C4, C5 stay queued, not scored.

### Nov 1, 2026, 3:15 PM CT: five ranked rule candidates for gold and Dogecoin (requested by main at the owner's direction; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY FORWARD SCORING; read only, nothing proposed live yet)

Source for every number: nightly report, `hours=2160&orders=1`, gold and Dogecoin filled and settled orders, 391 fills (gold 215, Dogecoin 176), after fees. In-sample and descriptive: they are where the ideas come from, NOT evidence for them (README P1: history cannot count toward a band or filter variant). Every rule below is scored forward on orders placed after this entry's time, with the old rule's orders as the comparison. N tried for deflation: about 728,654 before, plus about 24 cells looked at to write these (10 price cells, 10 rise3 cells, 4 exit and size cuts) = about 728,680; Bonferroni bar stays about z 5.2, so none of these can pass on a few dozen orders and each is a CHEAP QUESTION, not a finding. Frequency stays a hard requirement for L1: current rate is about 148 fills a week (last 14 days, gold plus Dogecoin).

What the live record says before any rule (so the owner sees the starting point):
- 391 fills, 18 losses. 16 of the 18 losses are early sells at the 25c line (gold 8, Dogecoin 8), total -$75.34, average -69c a contract. Only 2 losses were held to settlement, both gold. The loss side of this strategy IS the 25c line.
- Entry price paid (bought side), c per contract and losses: gold 88-90 +2.36 (4 of 35 lost), 90-92 -0.37 (5 of 47), 92-94 +3.04 (1 of 45), 94-96 +4.23 (0 of 54), 96-97 +2.79 (0 of 34). Dogecoin 88-90 +4.16 (2 of 25), 90-92 +0.33 (4 of 46), 92-94 +6.07 (0 of 33), 94-96 +4.17 (0 of 43), 96-97 -0.54 (2 of 29).
- Combined 90 to 92c: 93 fills, 9 losses, net -$1.01. Everything else: 298 fills, 9 losses, net +$66.43. One sided Fisher p 0.012 for the loss rate, but 10 price cells were looked at (gold and Dogecoin by 5 bins), so after counting the cells it is about 0.12. A lead.
- Recent three minute move of the bought side (`rise3`, logged since mid Oct, 259 fills): negative in 30 fills, 4 lost, net -$14.03. Non-negative in 229 fills, 8 lost, net +$55.29. Fisher p 0.038, about 0.2 after the 10 cells. A lead, small.

Ranked candidates (rank by what the live record supports and by frequency cost; the owner decides, one at a time):

**C1 (rank 1). Skip 90 to 92c entries, gold and Dogecoin.**
- Hypothesis: the 90 to 92c bucket is where the favourite is weakest relative to its price, so it pays about zero while it carries most of the loss events (9 of 18).
- Numeric prediction (forward): over the first 80 skipped-bucket orders, the bucket's net is under +0.5c a contract (live so far -0.37 gold, +0.33 Dogecoin, pooled about -0.1c) AND its loss rate is at least 7% (so far 9.7%) while the rest of the book stays under 4% (so far 3.0%). If both hold, skipping costs about 0 dollars of profit and removes about half the loss events.
- Frequency cost: about 24% of fills (76 of 297 in the last 14 days, so about 36 a week). This is the price, and it conflicts with "frequency is a hard requirement", so the owner must weigh it. It is the biggest cost of any candidate here.
- Kill rule: if the bucket's forward net is above +1.5c a contract at 80 orders, the candidate is dead (the dollars skipped would have been profit). If its loss rate is below 4% at 80 orders, dead.
- Needs: 80 forward 90 to 92c fills, about 2.2 weeks at 36 a week WITHOUT skipping them (scored on orders as sent, shadow only, no change live). Orders per week before: about 148. After: about 112.
- Risk: the cell was chosen after looking; a bucket that is bad in 391 can regress. Do not run live; score the existing orders as they arrive.

**C2 (rank 2). Skip an entry when the bought side's three minute move (`rise3`) is negative, gold and Dogecoin.**
- Hypothesis: a side that was falling into the 6 minute look is more likely to stop out at 25c (the first Dogecoin ladder loss had rise3 -7.6c).
- Numeric prediction: over the next 40 fills with rise3 below 0, at least 3 lose (live: 4 of 30), net under 0 (live -$14.03). If true, skipping them is worth about +$14 per 30 skipped and costs about 12% of fills (30 of 259; about 15 a week).
- Kill rule: at 40 forward negative-rise3 fills, if losses are 1 or fewer, or net is above +$2, dead. Also dead if V2's JUMP1 read (which owns rise3 research) comes back FALSIFIED on its own data.
- Needs: 40 forward fills with negative rise3, about 2.5 weeks at 15 a week. Orders per week before 148, after about 133.
- Overlap: V2 logged rise3 for JUMP1; coordinate through main so the two chats do not score the same split twice (count it once in N).

**C3 (rank 3). Dogecoin limit +1c (price improvement on no-fills), simulated first.**
- This is R2, promoted: Dogecoin ladder-size orders have a no-fill share of 35% (18 of 51); by limit price, below 92c 41% (13 of 32), 92 to 95c 29% (2 of 7), 95c and above 29% (4 of 14). A fill that is missed is a missed expectancy of about +2c a contract.
- Numeric prediction (simulation on R6 book data, no live change): a limit +1c converts at least a third of the no-fills into fills at a worse price by 1c; net change in expectancy per contract stays above +1.0c after the extra cent. Fills gained about +4 to +8 a week at the current rate.
- Kill rule: if the simulated net per extra fill is under +0.5c a contract after the extra cent, or the cap on price (the 97c band) removes the gain, dead.
- Needs: R6 Dogecoin days (since Oct 29) and an order export; no live probe yet. Orders per week before about 71 (Dogecoin), after about 75 to 79.

**C4 (rank 4). Lower the early-sell line from 25c to 15c on gold and Dogecoin (a safety setting; owner decision, not a research finding).**
- Evidence from the live record: 16 of 18 losses are exits, average -69c a contract, -$75.34 total. The research record (EXIT1, FALSIFIED as an improvement) says holding to settlement beats every exit shape in history (profit factor per contract hold 0.961 / 0.928 / 0.898 against live 25c line 0.921 / 0.897 / 0.869). That is HISTORY on all entries, not a live read, and it does not use this strategy's 88c to 97c band.
- Numeric prediction: with a 15c line, the exits that would have recovered above 25c at the close are at least 20% of the 16 (about 3); lines lower than 25c also raise the worst single loss from about -$9.5 toward -$11 at current size.
- Why it ranks 4 and not higher: it widens what a loss costs, the owner has called the 25c line the safety feature, and the live record cannot show the counterfactual (an exited position's market outcome is not in the nightly report). It needs V2 to count, for the 16 exited tickers, how many settled in the bought side's favour (R6 has market results only from Oct 29; the public market result can be read per ticker). No live probe until that count exists.
- Kill rule: if fewer than 3 of 16 exited positions settled in the bought side's favour, dead.
- Orders per week: unchanged (148). This changes loss size, not frequency.

**C5 (rank 5). Dogecoin upper band edge at 96c instead of 97c.**
- Dogecoin at 96 to 97c: 29 fills, 2 losses, net -$0.80 (-0.54c a contract) against +4.17c at 94 to 96 and +6.07 at 92 to 94. Gold at 96 to 97c is fine (+2.79c, 0 of 34). A 97c fill pays 3c to win and about 72c to lose when it is stopped, so one loss wipes out 24 wins.
- Numeric prediction: over the next 30 Dogecoin fills at 96 to 97c, net under +1.0c a contract and at least 1 loss (so far 2 of 29). If true, the cap costs about 8% of Dogecoin's fills (about 6 a week) for about zero dollars.
- Kill rule: at 30 forward fills, net above +2.5c a contract or 0 losses, dead.
- Needs 30 forward Dogecoin fills at 96 to 97c, about 5 weeks at 6 a week, so the slowest to read. Orders per week before about 71 (Dogecoin), after about 65.

Not proposed: sizing changes (the replay in `REFINE_SIZE_LADDER_REVIEW.md` shows no size effect I can prove) and the earlier-send plan (config only, waits on its own trigger).

What I need from the main session: the owner's pick of one candidate to score forward as a SHADOW read (no live change); V2 to count the 16 exit outcomes for C4 and to confirm how JUMP1 counts rise3 for C2. Nothing here goes live without the owner.

1. **Dogecoin order timing write-up is ready:** `kalshi-scalper/REFINE_TIMING_NOTES.md` (read only, no code changed). Short version: `ts` on an order is the moment the minute function started, so the 5 to 15 second "late" group is functions that started late (scheduler jitter or a cold start). After that, Dogecoin also waits for gold's whole order cycle (market read, record write, order, three more writes) because candidates are handled one at a time, gold first. The record has no send time, so the exact cost of each step is unknown. First step (zero risk): log send time on the order. Then keep-warm, parallel reads, moving after-order writes; sending both orders together is the riskiest (window cap and cash accounting are order by order today).

**2026-10-30 2:20 PM CT, R2 descriptive step (why attempts get no fill), from the new depth columns; 0 tries.** Rows with depth context since the columns exist: gold 111, Dogecoin 116 (the older rows have none). Outcome columns not used.

- **Gold: size against depth is NOT the cause.** 100 of 111 gold orders were under 5% of the depth at the touch, and 38% of those still got no fill. Gold depth is in the thousands (mean about 2,100 contracts), orders about 9.
- **Gold: lower price, more no-fills.** No fill share by the price the bot saw: 88c to 91c 50% (38 orders), 91c to 94c 35% (34), 94c to 97c 29% (37). Fits ask creep: a cheaper favourite is still moving up and leaves the limit behind; a 95c favourite is already priced.
- **Dogecoin: depth matters for large shares.** Order share of depth under 30%: 20% to 28% no fill (91 orders); 30% or more: 47% to 66% (25 orders). Depth is thin (mean about 100 contracts at the touch against orders of 3 to 5), so the 5 contract size and any ladder step above 5 are the places a no-fill will rise.
- **No partial fills at all:** 0 partial fills of 148 fills. Orders are all-or-nothing in practice, so a no-fill is a whole order lost, never half.
- **Prediction for the R2 registered proposal (limit +1c):** unchanged, FALSIFIED on dollars. The gold pattern (cheap, still rising favourites fail to fill) is exactly the set of orders that a +1c limit would fill, and no-fills have scored worse than fills (LIVE3, -0.82c vs +0.92c). For Dogecoin, depth is the other lever: ordering fewer contracts when share of depth would exceed 30% raises fills without raising price; that is a sizing rule and is not proposed because it cuts size.
- **Next for R2:** the simulation on R6 (fill if the ask 1 to 2 s later is within limit +1c) once R6 holds enough windows; needs V2's R6 clock rule. Nothing to the main session: no threshold.

**R1: edges of the band, 86c to 88c and 97c upper edge (gold and Dogecoin).**
- Hypothesis: more entries at the band edges without worse losers. Counterparty: none named (weakest part).
- Step 1, counts only (outcome columns hidden): windows per week at the 6 minute look with the fresh side price in 86 to <88c and in >97 to 98c, per market, on the 69 days; compared to the in-band count. 0 tries (counts).
- Step 2, registered test on fresh days only (close after this commit): net per contract after fee of the added cell and of 97c to 98c, 1 contract, held to settlement, taker at the ask. 2 tries (86 to 88, 97 to 98).
- Prediction: 86 to 88c net per contract under 0c (FALSIFIED); 97 to 98c under 0c (break-even needs a win rate above 98c plus fee; the 95 to 97c cell is already +0.17c net). Written before data.
- Kill: 150 fresh settled entries per cell, net per contract under +0.5c or loss rate above price paid plus fee: stop, no extension. Pass bar: the research bar (profit factor 1.5 in each of three windows after deflation); not expected to be reachable in two weeks of fresh days (about 25 entries a week per cell), so the likely verdict is NOT_ENOUGH_DATA.
- Fills per week: from the R6 count, 86 to 88c adds about +16 a week across both markets (range 0 to 40%); 97 to 98c to be counted.

**R2: why 28% to 35% of attempts get no fill, and a proposal that raises fills without worse losers.**
- Known: no-fills scored -0.82c a contract against fills +0.92c (LIVE3, held to settlement at the order limit), so a fill that raises fill rate by chasing price tends to ADD LOSERS (these are windows where the ask moved up). Ask creep before the order (README, 1.2 s tick delay) is the leading explanation. DEPTH1/DOGEFILL: size at the touch was above order size in the checked cases.
- Descriptive step (0 tries): from the nightly report and the order export (`depthAtLimit`, `shareOfDepth`, `entryLead`), split no-fills by order size, by gap between the attempt time and the touch read, by market. Needs a fresh Copy all orders export (the report has no depth fields). Information only.
- Registered proposal (1 try, fresh days, scored against plain L1 on the same windows): the order limit is the quoted ask PLUS 1c (still capped at 97c, same band). Prediction: fill rate rises by 8 to 15 points, but net per contract on the extra fills is under 0c, so the whole change is FALSIFIED on profit factor; the owner's frequency requirement is met by the extra fills, not the dollars. Kill: any 100 fresh extra fills with net per contract under +0.5c, or total loss rate above 8%.
- This is the same ground as V2's L1Q1 (resting entry); mine is the opposite direction (pay more, fill more). It needs a live probe to test properly (simulating it from the R6 book is possible: fill if the ask 1 to 2 s later is within limit +1c). I propose the R6 simulation first, no live order; brief for any live probe comes separately.

**2026-10-30 2:20 PM CT, Brief 1 count-only step done: the 86c to 88c cell adds about 8% (Dogecoin) to 17% (gold) more entries, from a very small R6 sample. No outcome column was read (book prices only; trades and results files untouched). 0 tries.**

Method: for each gold and Dogecoin 15 minute window in R6 (`book15r6`, Oct 29 22:03Z to Oct 30 about 12:43Z files on `quote-data`), take the book poll nearest 360 seconds before the close (within 30 seconds); a side's ask is 1 minus the other side's best bid; a window counts as L1 (88c to 97c inclusive, either side) or as the added cell (86c up to but not including 88c, neither side in 88c to 97c).

| Market | Windows with a 6 minute poll | L1 band (88c to 97c) | Added by 86c to 88c | Increase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Gold | 41 | 12 | 2 | +17% |
| Dogecoin | 45 | 12 | 1 | +8% |

Applied to the live fill rate (about 61 gold and 71 Dogecoin fills a week over Oct 16 to 30): roughly +10 gold and +6 Dogecoin fills a week, about +16 a week in total, of about 132. That range is wide: the counts are 1 and 2 added windows, so the true increase could be anywhere from near 0% to 40%. Also not allowed for: the live no-fill share (35% gold, 28% Dogecoin) would apply to the added orders too, and the live rule uses the fresh quote at the bot's look, not the poll nearest 360 seconds.

Not done: the 69 day candle history is not in this container (`kalshi-scalper/data` is not committed), so I could not give the longer count. If the owner wants it, the research session or main session can run the same count there; it is still a count with outcomes hidden. Read plainly: even on the upper figure, the cell adds under 20 fills a week and the evidence nearest to it (88c to 90c net -0.36c) says those fills lose cents, so the case for widening on frequency alone is weak.

**2026-10-30 2:00 PM CT, four band and frequency briefs for the owner to approve (owner request via the main session). Nothing has been run on any data.**

Baseline I am measuring against (live, Oct 16 to Oct 30, two weeks, from the nightly report): gold 122 fills, about 61 a week (35% of attempts got no fill); Dogecoin 143 fills, about 71 a week (28% no fill). So L1 today is about 130 fills a week on the two markets, 6 minute entry only. Net per contract live: gold +2.34c, Dogecoin +2.09c (30 days).

Fixed rules for all four: gold and Dogecoin only, both with identical rules; public recorders only (R6 15 minute books from Oct 29, and the quote recorder on `quote-data`); only markets that close AFTER the freeze timestamp I write into the registration commit before any row is read; the 69 days of history cannot count toward a band variant (README P1), so history is used only for the count of entries, never for the verdict. Deflation: README N is about 627,838 (V2, Oct 30); these four add 4 tries, plus 1 for the 86c to 88c sub-split if it is read as two cells. Bar is the research bar already in CLAUDE.md: profit factor 1.5 or better after fees in each of three windows, deflated for N; I do not expect any of these to reach it in the first read, and they are not promotable on that alone (nothing here has a promote verb). Information needed from the owner for a live probe is a separate, later decision.

**Brief 1: lower edge 88c to 86c (add the 86c to 88c cell).**
- Hypothesis: the 86c to 88c cell is a continuation of the band's cheap end and adds fills.
- Counterparty: none named. This is the weakest part of the case.
- Prediction (written before any read): the added cell nets under 0c a contract after fees, FALSIFIED. Reason: the known history already has 88c to 90c at gross +0.35c and net -0.36c (n 518), and the trend into cheaper prices is down, with break-even needing a win rate above the price paid plus fee.
- Kill: after 150 fresh settled entries in 86c to 88c, net per contract under +0.5c or a loss rate above (price paid + fee); stop at 150, no extension.
- Orders per week: unknown until counted. Cost-free step I propose first, with owner approval: COUNT entries with outcomes hidden from the first look on the 69 day candles and the R6 files (a count, not a result). Expect roughly 15% to 25% more entries (my guess from the 88c to 90c cell being the thinnest-priced cell; stated as a guess, to be replaced by the count).
- Plain statement: widening adds orders but the nearest evidence says the added orders lose. Frequency is bought with expected dollars here.

**Brief 2: a second look at 8 or 10 minutes for entries missed at 6.**
- Hypothesis: a market inside the band at 8 or 10 minutes left that has left the band by 6 minutes (or never fills) can be taken earlier, adding fills without changing the rule.
- Counterparty: none named. Earlier evidence is mixed: natural gas +3.19c at 10 minutes vs +0.52c at 6, XRP +2.91c vs +1.47c, but pooled over 18 series 10 minutes missed its bar (z 1.71), and XRP's live 10 minute entries lost 12 of 75 (-$7.20). Gold at the 10 minute look was 0.89 profit factor (GOLD1D).
- Prediction: gold FALSIFIED (net per contract under 0 at 10 minutes, consistent with GOLD1D); Dogecoin not-passing (below the 6 minute look, PF within 0.1 of 1.0). I expect the cleanest result to be "adds fills, loses cents".
- Kill: any look must show net per contract at least +0.5c on 100 fresh entries, and the loss rate must stay under 8%.
- Orders per week: from the TWOSTAGE log already on every live order (`ask10`, `inBand10`, `ask8`, `inBand8`, `twoStageLeft`), the count of markets in band at 8 or 10 minutes but with no L1 fill can be read from the order export with no new live data. Needs the owner to paste a fresh Copy all orders export; not available to me.
- Plain statement: V2 owns the earlier-entry family (Oct 28 closure of L1 filter, exit and sizing tests); this brief stays only because the owner asked, and I lean toward not running it.

**Brief 3: either side vs favourite-only.**
- Hypothesis: L1 already takes whichever side is in the band, so this is about the market where both sides are out of band at the same time. I could not find a rule to extend, so this is a clarification question for the owner and the main session: is the live rule "the side whose price is in the 88c to 97c band" (so both sides is impossible, one side is always at most 12c) or "the favourite only"? If the former, there is nothing to add and this brief is withdrawn. I am not spending a try on it until that is answered.
- Orders per week: zero change under either reading unless the band is also widened.

**Brief 4: a filter that adds orders without adding losers.**
- I could not name one. Every filter tested so far REMOVES entries (REGIME1, NEWH5, MKTTIME1, the smooth climb gate) and the only information-only effect found is that entries with a spread of 2c or more do worse (removed entries 0.798 / 0.753 / 0.716 in REGIME1), which cuts count. The honest answer to "adds orders without adding losers" is that fills come from the other end: the 35% (gold) and 28% (Dogecoin) of attempts that got no fill.
- Alternative I propose instead (counts as 1 try): L1Q1 and MAKER5 are already registered by V2 on R6 (resting entry instead of paying the ask, read from Nov 6). If resting entries raise the fill rate without worse losers, that is the one live frequency lever with a mechanism (the no-fill share is order count thrown away). I recommend waiting for L1Q1 rather than registering a duplicate.
- Kill and prediction belong to L1Q1's registration; I will quote its read when it runs.

**My recommendation:** approve only the count-only step of Brief 1 (no outcomes, 0 tries) and the question in Brief 3; skip Brief 2 and Brief 4 as written because V2's L1Q1/MAKER5 already cover the only frequency lever with a mechanism. If the owner values frequency above expected cents, Brief 1 is the only one that adds orders, and its predicted result is negative, so it would be a deliberate cost.
